# Paper numbers (single source)

Generated by `experiments/log_parsing_paper_numbers.py`. Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, log parsing on Loghub-2.0, rules_template, strict match, 13 systems (Proxifier dropped). Main setting **fair_leakfree**: Clotho with the paper's balanced sampling, layer 16 for every hidden-state method, label from samples 5-9 (PASS if >= 3 of 5 exact matches), post-generation scores from samples 0-4 only. Unless a section says otherwise, every number is ROC-AUC (PASS = positive class), **mean ± std over 5 seeds of the per-seed macro mean over systems**. Reference size counts the 10 initial tests. Splits: standard = Clotho's protocol; disjoint = no Loghub template shared between the reference pool and the test set. * = needs the generations (post-generation). Sections 1-8 are Llama; section 9 repeats the core comparison and the natural check for Gemma-2-9B-it.

Reading: on the standard split Clotho (0.749 / 0.794) beats every other pre-generation method and every lookup; leakage-free output entropy ties or beats it (0.792 / 0.794), and the rank average of the two is the best score (0.826 / 0.845). On the template-disjoint split every lookup is at chance, Clotho and MDSA tie (0.645-0.657), and output entropy keeps 0.796.

### Paired per-seed differences: output entropy(k) minus Clotho

| k | standard @100 | standard @200 | disjoint @100 | disjoint @200 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | -0.084 ± 0.011 | -0.128 ± 0.013 | +0.016 ± 0.022 | +0.004 ± 0.013 |
| 3 | -0.014 ± 0.011 | -0.060 ± 0.012 | +0.086 ± 0.020 | +0.075 ± 0.012 |

Smallest k with mean output-entropy AUC >= Clotho: standard @100: 4, standard @200: > 5, disjoint @100: 2, disjoint @200: 2.

Reading: output entropy needs 4 samples to match Clotho at reference size 100 on the standard split (5 samples only tie it at 200; on the template-disjoint split 2 samples already tie it and 3 beat it by about +0.08). Generating 4 samples costs 2.4x Clotho's batched HF forward pass, 8.3x a vLLM prefill (the same engine), and 36x online (batch 1). Clotho + output entropy (k=5) is the best score at about 4x Clotho's batched cost.

## 6. Leakage

Original protocol (code reading; `reports/log_parsing_leakage_original.md`, upstream commit e8aed3d):

**Answer: yes.** Each input has one generation run (10 samples, T=0.8). It writes one JSON file, and that file supplies both the label (fraction of the 10 samples that pass) and every post-generation metric. LOHSvar's output hidden states are recorded during that same run. Tok-Prob and Tok-Ent use sample 0, which is also one of the 10 samples behind the label. Sem-Ent and LOHSvar use all 10.

Measured effect in our setting (script `log_parsing_phase3_final.py report`; leaky = label and scores share all 10 samples; `net` subtracts the shift of methods that cannot see the generations):

`net` = diff minus the mean diff of the methods that never see the generations (pre-generation and lookup: +0.024 @100, +0.021 @200). Those methods move only because the leakage-free label is noisier (5 samples, >= 3 of 5) and has a different pass rate, so `net` is the part of the change attributable to leakage.

| method | leaky @100 | leakage-free @100 | diff @100 | net @100 | leaky @200 | leakage-free @200 | diff @200 | net @200 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Lookup (Drain, default) | 0.711 ± 0.008 | 0.694 ± 0.003 | +0.017 | -0.007 | 0.779 ± 0.008 | 0.750 ± 0.004 | +0.030 | +0.008 |
| Lookup (oracle) | 0.708 ± 0.009 | 0.691 ± 0.005 | +0.017 | -0.007 | 0.778 ± 0.006 | 0.749 ± 0.003 | +0.029 | +0.007 |

| Random | 0.505 ± 0.012 | 0.504 ± 0.004 | +0.001 | -0.022 | 0.500 ± 0.010 | 0.506 ± 0.010 | -0.006 | -0.028 |

Reading: in the original protocol labels and post-generation metrics share one set of 10 generations. In our setting this inflates output entropy by +0.125 / +0.132 AUC (net), LOHSvar by +0.067 / +0.070 and Sem-Ent by +0.040 / +0.038; Tok-Prob/Tok-Ent move by at most +0.011 net.

## 7. Label noise vs within-template signal (40 extra samples per input)

Scripts: `log_parsing_extra_samples.py` (vLLM, engine seed 1), `log_parsing_label_noise.py` -> `reports/log_parsing_label_noise.md`.

Median within-template reliability of the 40-sample pass rate: 0.56 (overall: 0.97).

Cells: mean ± std over seeds of the macro mean over systems with >= 20 same-template PASS/FAIL pairs on every seed. Overall AUC is over the same systems.

| method | within @100 | overall @100 | within @200 | overall @200 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Clotho | 0.609 ± 0.024 | 0.832 ± 0.022 | 0.571 ± 0.028 | 0.850 ± 0.018 |
| MDSA | 0.557 ± 0.007 | 0.765 ± 0.016 | 0.532 ± 0.017 | 0.795 ± 0.012 |
| LOHSvar | 0.562 ± 0.014 | 0.663 ± 0.004 | 0.570 ± 0.017 | 0.658 ± 0.009 |
| Output entropy | 0.584 ± 0.010 | 0.810 ± 0.003 | 0.568 ± 0.012 | 0.801 ± 0.006 |
| Noise ceiling: pass rate on the original 10 samples | 0.698 ± 0.005 | 0.939 ± 0.002 | 0.664 ± 0.008 | 0.917 ± 0.006 |
| Lookup (oracle) | 0.500 ± 0.000 | 0.823 ± 0.010 | 0.500 ± 0.000 | 0.873 ± 0.013 |

Systems: 7 @100 (apache, hdfs, hpc, healthapp, openssh, openstack, zookeeper), 6 @200.

Same systems, noisy vs clean label:

| method | within @100 noisy | within @100 clean | within @200 noisy | within @200 clean |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Clotho | 0.555 ± 0.017 | 0.609 ± 0.024 | 0.524 ± 0.010 | 0.571 ± 0.028 |
| MDSA | 0.558 ± 0.006 | 0.557 ± 0.007 | 0.552 ± 0.011 | 0.532 ± 0.017 |
| LOHSvar | 0.545 ± 0.005 | 0.562 ± 0.014 | 0.534 ± 0.003 | 0.570 ± 0.017 |
| Output entropy | 0.529 ± 0.005 | 0.584 ± 0.010 | 0.514 ± 0.008 | 0.568 ± 0.012 |

Reading: near-chance within-template AUC is mostly label noise on top of a small real signal. 95-99% of the reliable variance of an input's pass rate lies between templates on 11 of 13 systems (Mac and Thunderbird trivially, with one input per template; HPC 0.80, OpenSSH 0.86), and even the 40-sample pass rate has a median within-template reliability of only 0.56, so a 5-sample within-template label is mostly sampling noise. Against the clean 40-sample label, within-template AUC rises on the same systems and test sets (Clotho 0.555 -> 0.609 @100, output entropy 0.529 -> 0.584; MDSA unchanged at 0.557), against a ceiling of 0.698 for perfect knowledge of the original 10-sample pass rate. So Clotho does carry some within-template signal, but it is small next to the between-template signal (overall AUC 0.832 on the same systems).

Reading: sampled at their natural frequency, 500 lines cover 5-120 templates per system (the suites: 29-500), and at @100 the Drain group of 59-100% of test inputs (100% on 7 of 13 systems) already occurs in the reference set. Every reference-based score rises: Clotho 0.749 -> 0.831 @100 and 0.794 -> 0.845 @200, MDSA +0.10, Lookup (Drain) most (0.680 -> 0.838, 0.733 -> 0.861). Clotho's lead over Lookup (Drain) on the suites (+0.069 / +0.061) vanishes: -0.006 ± 0.014 @100 and -0.016 ± 0.014 @200 (Clotho ahead on 6 and 4 of 13 systems). Its lead over MDSA shrinks to +0.016 / +0.007. Output entropy ignores the reference set and stays at 0.789 / 0.784, so Clotho now beats it on every seed (+0.042 / +0.061; smallest per-seed gap +0.023), the reverse of the suites at @100. Clotho + output entropy is the best score at @100 (0.854, +0.016 ± 0.011 over Lookup (Drain), positive on every seed) and ties the lookup at @200 (-0.005 ± 0.007). Stage 8's 4-system result (`reports/log_parsing_natural.md`) holds on all 13 systems: on the natural distribution, a Drain lookup over labelled reference lines matches Clotho, and Clotho's value over cheap lookups lies in templates the reference set has not seen (the template-capped suites and the disjoint split). The post-generation baselines added on 2026-11-09 stay well below Clotho on the natural samples: LOHSvar 0.685 / 0.696 (Clotho ahead by +0.146 / +0.149 on every seed) and Tok-Prob 0.504 / 0.492 (at chance).

## 9. Second model: Gemma-2-9B-it (same protocol)

Same rules_template prompt, strict scorer, label rule and layer-tuning procedure as for Llama; nothing was re-tuned. Generation with vLLM (prompt/token parity with `clotho/models/gemma.py` checked before every run). PASS rates and drops: `reports/log_parsing_pass_rates_gemma.md` (Apache dropped at 94.4% PASS under the > 5 of 10 rule; Proxifier not generated, as for Llama), so **12 systems**. Layer tuning on HDFS, Spark, Linux (jobs in RESULTS_REPO `results_phase3_gemma/tuning/`; mean AUC under balanced: layer 14 0.758, **21 0.773**, 28 0.765, 35 0.769) chose **layer 21** of 42 (half depth, as Llama's 16 of 32). Reports: `*_gemma.md`.

### 9.7 Label noise (40 extra samples, engine seed 1; `log_parsing_label_noise.py`, 2026-11-09 night)

Median within-template reliability of the 40-sample pass rate: 0.76 (overall: 0.99).
| method | within @100 noisy | within @100 clean | within @200 noisy | within @200 clean |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Clotho | 0.558 ± 0.014 | 0.594 ± 0.017 | 0.547 ± 0.016 | 0.588 ± 0.008 |
| MDSA | 0.515 ± 0.009 | 0.523 ± 0.025 | 0.517 ± 0.022 | 0.516 ± 0.038 |
| LOHSvar | 0.526 ± 0.009 | 0.568 ± 0.010 | 0.536 ± 0.003 | 0.587 ± 0.019 |
| Output entropy | 0.554 ± 0.010 | 0.588 ± 0.008 | 0.549 ± 0.010 | 0.605 ± 0.013 |


Reading: every Llama finding about the pre-generation methods and lookups holds for Gemma-2-9B on 12 systems. On the standard split Clotho (0.780 / 0.832) beats MDSA (+0.063 / +0.095) and Lookup (Drain) (+0.074 / +0.058) on every seed, also on the 9 held-out systems and with random reference sets for the baselines; on the template-disjoint split the lookups fall to chance and Clotho ties MDSA (+0.004 / +0.010). Leakage-free output entropy (0.821 / 0.818) beats Clotho at @100 (-0.040) but Clotho is ahead at @200 on every seed (+0.014, smallest +0.006); output entropy again needs 4 samples to match Clotho at @100, and Clotho + output entropy is the best score (0.869 / 0.890). Across systems Clotho and MDSA are again close (Clotho minus MDSA -0.025 / +0.003). On the natural samples the Drain lookup beats Clotho on every seed (-0.037 ± 0.018 @100, -0.057 ± 0.009 @200), a larger gap than for Llama, and Clotho beats output entropy by about 0.1 (+0.108 / +0.093); LOHSvar (0.669 / 0.662) and Tok-Prob (0.478 / 0.473) stay far below Clotho there too. The other post-generation baselines stay well below Clotho on the standard split (LOHSvar 0.677 / 0.672, Sem-Ent 0.617 / 0.612, Tok-Ent 0.578 / 0.575, Tok-Prob 0.486 / 0.480). Leakage again inflates the post-generation scores (net output entropy +0.064 / +0.066, Sem-Ent +0.025 / +0.029, LOHSvar +0.022 / +0.020), less than for Llama. With Gemma timings on the same L40S, the 4 samples output entropy needs to match Clotho at @100 cost 1.5x Clotho's batched HF forward pass (layers 1-21), 6.9x a vLLM prefill and 22x online (batch 1).

## 10. Paper artefacts

`experiments/paper/`: one script per LaTeX table (`tables/*.tex`, booktabs) and figure (`figures/*.pdf`), all reading the same per-job JSON as the sections above (`paper/common.py`). Tables: `table_main.py` (both models, capped vs natural, standard split), `table_disjoint.py`, `table_leakage.py`, `table_cross.py`, `table_literature.py` (A, B). Figures: `fig_cost_auc.py`, `fig_clotho_vs_lookup.py`, `fig_within_template.py`, `fig_budget.py` (C), `fig_transfer.py` (D, paired), `fig_triage.py` (TRIAGE E1); tables `table_triage_ablation.py` (E2), `table_e5.py` (E5 and G); `fig_strong_gap.py` (F3), `fig_lorenz.py` (N1), `fig_difficulty.py` (L), `fig_crossmodel.py` (J), `table_downstream.py` (H, N3), `table_unseen.py` (U, V), `fig_groupcheck.py` (W). Bold = best in its column and ahead of the runner-up on every seed. RQ outline: `paper/results_outline.md`.

The corrector fixes 339 of 4221 failing inputs (8.0%) and breaks 97 of 2279 passing ones. Wall-clock is batched vLLM on the L40S (all outputs of a system submitted per correction round, prefix caching on, engine start excluded).

Cost: the corrector makes 0.39 LLM calls per output (1.95 per input for the 5 label samples) and takes 0.0122 s per output, 0.061 s per input. Clotho's batched HF forward pass to layer 16 plus GMM scoring takes 0.0202 s per input (`results_phase3/timing/hf.json`), one call and no generation: the corrector costs 0.6x Clotho per corrected output. Neither includes generating the output itself.

The corrector fixes 210 of 3549 failing inputs (5.9%) and breaks 219 of 2451 passing ones. Wall-clock is batched vLLM on the L40S (all outputs of a system submitted per correction round, prefix caching on, engine start excluded).

Cost: the corrector makes 0.34 LLM calls per output (1.68 per input for the 5 label samples) and takes 0.0094 s per output, 0.047 s per input. Clotho's batched HF forward pass to layer 21 plus GMM scoring takes 0.0351 s per input (`results_phase3_gemma/timing/hf.json`), one call and no generation: the corrector costs 0.3x Clotho per corrected output. Neither includes generating the output itself.

Predictors against the corrected label (11 systems; skipped: Linux (10 PASS / 490 FAIL under the corrected label)). Same systems under the original label for comparison.

Reading: LLMLog's selection score ranks failures below Clotho on every seed for both models and suites (0.620-0.720 vs 0.749-0.880 @100), almost entirely through its confidence part; its representativeness part is at chance. AdaParser's corrector fixes 5.9-15.6% of failing inputs and breaks 97-219 passing ones; against the corrected label Clotho keeps or raises its AUC, and the failures in its top 10% are the least fixable (2.1-4.9% fixed vs 6.0-15.3% of all test failures).

Within-system Clotho with k labels could fit (>= 10 passing references, incl. initial tests) on all seeds for 100% of targets at k = 10, 100% of targets at k = 25, 100% of targets at k = 50; otherwise random.

Reading (held-out targets): pooled-source Clotho stays at 0.44-0.66; per-system normalisation and CORAL do not help; a logistic probe on source labels reaches 0.67-0.71 without target labels; refitting the best source model with 25-50 target labels matches within-system Clotho @100 (not paired). Generic log features alone reach 0.62-0.68. The within-system Clotho rows of this section received the 0/1 label and unpaired test sets; section 14 supersedes them (continuous label score, as every other Clotho run, and identical target inputs).

#### Cost per new template (macro mean over systems)

AdaParser corrector (vLLM, batched over the system's templates): 56 ms; greedy parse (vLLM, batched): 43 ms; hidden state as a separate HF forward pass (unbatched): 59 ms (free if the parser's prefill exposes it); TRIAGE CPU (prior PCA + 20 refits + queue for 100 reviews, one process) per template: 2 ms.

Output entropy = entropy of the parsed answers (task labeler) over the samples; it is not the stored `answer_level_entropy` (present for some tasks only; Spearman with ours: GH-TYPO -0.45, JSON-FIX -0.39, POS-TAG -0.05, TOPIC-CLS -0.22, ODD-ADD -0.25, MODEL-EX -0.15). MDSA coverage (inputs with a stored score): SYN-BUG 0.99, SPELL-CHK 0.98, GH-TYPO 0.98, JSON-FIX 0.31, POS-TAG 0.24, TOPIC-CLS 0.98, ODD-ADD 0.97, MODEL-EX 0.62

## 30. CPU round 3 robustness (partial)

CPU only: stored outputs, hidden states and stream caches; no new LLM calls or model/tokenizer downloads. Protocols and deviations are in PROGRESS Decisions. Seeds 0–4; mean ± sample std of seed macro means. Held-out excludes HDFS, Spark and Linux. Llama base uses 11 systems (HDFS/OpenSSH caches missing); strong Llama 13, Gemma 12.

## R1. Does scoring convention explain the conclusions?

**The 20-review frequency claim changes under lenient scoring:** D x size beats Most frequent on all five seeds for gemma/base/L1, gemma/base/L2.

“Close to Oracle” is quantified below, rather than assumed. L1/L2 are sensitivity analyses, not a new ground truth.

| model/parser | scorer | systems | PA_0 | Most frequent / Oracle @20 | D minus frequency @20 (positive seeds) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| llama/base | strict | 11 | 0.255 | 0.977 | -0.011 ± 0.016 (2/5) |
| llama/base | L1 | 11 | 0.362 | 0.962 | -0.026 ± 0.035 (2/5) |
| llama/base | L2 | 11 | 0.363 | 0.962 | -0.013 ± 0.028 (1/5) |
| llama/strong | strict | 13 | 0.639 | 0.964 | -0.020 ± 0.004 (0/5) |
| llama/strong | L1 | 13 | 0.696 | 0.954 | -0.022 ± 0.004 (0/5) |
| llama/strong | L2 | 13 | 0.696 | 0.954 | -0.024 ± 0.004 (0/5) |
| gemma/base | strict | 12 | 0.364 | 0.966 | -0.019 ± 0.000 (0/5) |
| gemma/base | L1 | 12 | 0.424 | 0.960 | 0.008 ± 0.000 (5/5) |
| gemma/base | L2 | 12 | 0.438 | 0.950 | 0.005 ± 0.000 (5/5) |
| gemma/strong | strict | 12 | 0.630 | 0.960 | -0.007 ± 0.000 (0/5) |
| gemma/strong | L1 | 12 | 0.672 | 0.954 | -0.003 ± 0.000 (0/5) |
| gemma/strong | L2 | 12 | 0.673 | 0.953 | -0.002 ± 0.000 (0/5) |

Suite PASS rates increase under L1/L2. The disjoint U-best advantage over Clotho remains; full AUC, variance-share, N1 and N5 results are in the details.

## 31. Real AdaParser: candidate provenance and restricted review queues

Source: `reports/adaparser_e2e_review.md`; protocol in PROGRESS.md Decisions.


The official parser preloads 32 labelled Loghub-2.0 candidates per system; provenance shares count only templates with stream lines. Strict scoring; seeds 0–4; mean ± sample std of per-seed system macro means. Template failures use the first-line verdict, and a majority correction fixes one only when it equals that first-line GT. Line and template Oracles optimize their own metric. Parser cache seed 0; seeds 0–4 apply to review policies. Candidate/LLM/mixed/unknown provenance tracks final trie ancestry. LLM-only excludes mixed groups; LLM-involved includes them. Both restrictions exclude unknowns and refit within their own queue. Budgets are capped at the cohort’s available templates; actual review counts are saved per run.
